that is unbounded and the algorithm does not converge with a
1/t
rate to
\mathfrak{m}=\int\phi(x)\,dP(x)\in\mathcal{H}
.

Proof.

(a) Definition of the convex set:

Let
\{N_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
be a set of natural numbers to be defined below, pick a countable infinite orthonormal sequence
\{e_{n}^{\prime}\}_{n\geq 1}
in
\mathcal{H}
and split this sequence into

\{e_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
and the sequences
\tilde{e}_{n,1},\ldots,\tilde{e}_{n,N_{n}}
where
n
goes through
2,3\ldots
. This can be done since these are countable many sequences of
N_{n}+1
elements and since countable unions of countable sets are again countable.
Furthermore, define the sequences
\{a_{n}\}_{n\geq 1},\{b_{n}\}_{n\geq 1},\{c_{n,m}:1\leq n,1\leq m\leq N_{n}\},\{d_{n}:2\leq n\}\subseteq\mathcal{H}
by

\displaystyle a_{n}

\displaystyle:=\left(a_{n}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{n}\right)e_{n}\text{ with }a_{n}^{\prime}:=C\left\lceil\frac{2^{n}}{\ln(n+1)}\right\rceil 2^{-n}\text{ and }C=4\left\lceil 3+\frac{4\ln(9)}{\ln(2)}\right\rceil=64,

\displaystyle b_{n}

\displaystyle:=-2^{-n}e_{n},

\displaystyle N_{1}

\displaystyle:=1\text{ and for }n\geq 2,N_{n}:=\left\lceil\frac{2}{n\left\langle-b_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle}\right\rceil,

\displaystyle c_{1,1}

\displaystyle:=e_{1}+\alpha_{2,1}\tilde{e}_{2,1},\text{ and for }n\geq 2:

\displaystyle c_{n,1}

\displaystyle:=\beta_{n}e_{n}+\alpha_{n,1}\tilde{e}_{n,1}-\alpha_{n,2}\tilde{e}_{n,2},\,\quad\ldots

\displaystyle c_{n,N_{n}-1}

\displaystyle:=\beta_{n}e_{n}+\alpha_{n,N_{n}-1}\tilde{e}_{n,N_{n}-1}-\alpha_{n,N_{n}}\tilde{e}_{n,N_{n}},

\displaystyle c_{n,N_{n}}

\displaystyle:=\beta_{n}e_{n}+\alpha_{n,N_{n}}\tilde{e}_{n,N_{n}}-\alpha_{n+1,1}\tilde{e}_{n+1,1},

\displaystyle d_{2}

\displaystyle:=-(1/2)\alpha_{2,1}\tilde{e}_{2,1}\text{ and for all }2\leq n\text{ let }

\displaystyle d_{n}

\displaystyle:=(1/2)\alpha_{n,1}\tilde{e}_{n,1},

\displaystyle\beta_{n}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{nN_{n}},\text{ for which }-\beta_{n}<\left\langle-b_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle\text{ holds,}

\displaystyle\alpha_{n,1}

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{\frac{\left\langle e_{n},a_{n}\right\rangle}{n}},\text{ and }\alpha_{n,i}:=\sqrt{\alpha_{n,1}^{2}+(i-1)\beta^{2}_{n}}\text{ for }2\leq i\leq N_{n}.

-\beta_{n}
is smaller than
\left\langle-b_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle
because

-\beta_{n}=1/(nN_{n})\leq\left\langle-b_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle/2
. Also observe that the sequence
a_{n}^{\prime}
is non-increasing in
n
since

\displaystyle\left\lceil\frac{2^{n+1}}{\ln(n+2)}\right\rceil\frac{1}{2^{n+1}}\leq\left\lceil 2\frac{2^{n}}{\ln(n+1)}\right\rceil\frac{1}{2^{n+1}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lceil 2\left\lceil\frac{2^{n}}{\ln(n+1)}\right\rceil\right\rceil\frac{1}{2^{n+1}}=\left\lceil\frac{2^{n}}{\ln(n+1)}\right\rceil\frac{1}{2^{n}},

where we used that the function
\lceil\cdot\rceil
is monotonically increasing.

(b) Construction of a continuous map
\phi
:

We construct a continuous function

\phi:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
which goes through the points
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}},\{b_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
,
\{c_{n,i}:1\leq n,1\leq i\leq N_{n}\}

and
\{d_{n}:2\leq n\}
. We split the construction into three separate functions,
\phi_{1}
for the
a_{n},b_{n}
elements,
\phi_{2}
for the
c_{n.i}
and
\phi_{3}
for the
d_{n}
elements.

For ease of reading let
y_{n}=1/(n+1)
and

z_{n}=(y_{n}+y_{n+1})/2
for all
n\geq 1
. Define
\phi_{1}:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
, with n going through
1,2,3\ldots
, by

\phi_{1}(x):=\begin{cases}\frac{1-x}{1-y_{1}}\,a_{1}&\text{if }y_{1}<x\leq 1,\\
\frac{x-\xi}{y_{n}-\xi}\,a_{n}&\text{if }\xi:=\frac{y_{n}+z_{n}}{2}<x\leq y_{n},\\
\frac{\xi-x}{\xi-z_{n}}\,b_{n}&\text{if }z_{n}<x\leq\frac{y_{n}+z_{n}}{2}=:\xi,\\
\frac{x-\xi}{z_{n}-\xi}\,b_{n}&\text{if }\xi:=\frac{y_{n+1}+z_{n}}{2}<x\leq z_{n},\\
\frac{\xi-x}{\xi-y_{n+1}}\,a_{n+1}&\text{if }y_{n+1}<x\leq\frac{y_{n+1}+z_{n}}{2}=:\xi,\\
0&\text{if }x=0.\end{cases}

The function is continuous on
(0,1]
as it is piecewise linear and the
end points of the lines are connected. The only critical point is
0
. For continuity at
0
it suffices that
for any
\epsilon>0
we can pick a
\delta
such that
x<\delta
implies

\left\lVert\phi(x)\right\rVert<\epsilon
. We restrict the search for a
\delta
to points
1/n
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
. For such a
\delta
the maximum of
\phi(x)
in an interval
[0,\delta]
is either attained on an
a_{n}
or a
b_{n}
. As we have that

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\left\lVert a_{n}\right\rVert=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\left\lVert b_{n}\right\rVert=0

there is for every
\epsilon>0
an
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all
n>N

we have
\max(\left\lVert a_{n}\right\rVert,\left\lVert b_{n}\right\rVert)<\epsilon
and, consequently for

\delta=1/(N+1)
we have that
\left\lVert\phi(x)\right\rVert<\epsilon
for any
0\leq x\leq\delta
.

In the following let
N_{1}:=1
. Furthermore, let
\tilde{y}_{n}:=1/n
,
\Delta_{n}:=(\tilde{y}_{n}-\tilde{y}_{n+1})/N_{n}
,
u_{n,m}:=\tilde{y}_{n}-m\Delta_{n}
,
u_{n,0}:=\tilde{y}_{n}
and let

\tilde{z}_{n,m}:=(u_{n,m-1}+u_{n,m})/2
, for all
n\geq 1
and
1\leq m\leq N_{n}-1
.
With
n
going through all of
1,2,\ldots
, define
\phi_{2}:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
by

\phi_{2}(x):=\begin{cases}\frac{\tilde{y}_{n}-x}{\tilde{y}_{n}-\tilde{z}_{n,1}}\,c_{n,1}&\text{if }\tilde{z}_{n,1}<x\leq\tilde{y}_{n},\\
\frac{x-u_{n,m}}{\tilde{z}_{n,m}-u_{n,m}}\,c_{n,m}&\text{if }u_{n,m}<x\leq\tilde{z}_{n,m},1\leq m\leq N_{n}-1,\\
\frac{u_{n,m}-x}{u_{n,m}-\tilde{z}_{n,m+1}}\,c_{n,m+1}&\text{if }\tilde{z}_{n,m+1}<x\leq u_{n,m},1\leq m\leq N_{n}-1,\\
\frac{x-\tilde{y}_{n+1}}{\tilde{z}_{n,N_{n}}-\tilde{y}_{n+1}}\,c_{n,N_{n}}&\text{if }\tilde{y}_{n+1}<x\leq\tilde{z}_{n,N_{n}},\\
0&\text{if }x=0.\end{cases}

Similarly, by going through all
n\geq 2
define

\phi_{3}:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
by

\phi_{3}(x):=\begin{cases}0&\text{if }1/2<x\leq 1,\\
\frac{\tilde{y}_{n}-x}{\tilde{y}_{n}-\tilde{z}_{n,1}}\,d_{n}&\text{if }\tilde{z}_{n,1}<x\leq\tilde{y}_{n},\\
\frac{x-u_{n,1}}{\tilde{z}_{n,1}-u_{n,1}}\,d_{n}&\text{if }u_{n,1}<x\leq\tilde{z}_{n,1},\\
0&\tilde{y}_{n+1}\leq x\leq u_{n,1},\\
0&\text{if }x=0.\end{cases}

With the same reasoning as for
\phi_{1}
one can infer that
\phi_{2}
and
\phi_{3}
are continuous.
Define

\phi(x):=\begin{cases}\phi_{1}(3x-2)&\text{if }2/3<x\leq 1,\\
\phi_{2}(3x-1)&\text{if }1/3<x\leq 2/3,\\
\phi_{3}(3x)&\text{if }0\leq x\leq 1/3.\\
\end{cases}

The function
\phi
is continuous since
\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3}
are continuous and
\phi_{1}(0)=\phi_{2}(1)=\phi_{2}(0)=\phi_{3}(1)=0
. This implies also that
\phi:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
is measurable. It is also Bochner integrable with respect to any probability measure defined on the Borel sets of
\mathbb{R}
as
\left\lVert\phi(\cdot)\right\rVert:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is continuous and, hence, bounded, i.e.
\int\left\lVert\phi(x)\right\rVert\,dP(x)<\infty
.

(c) Definition of the probability measure:

We construct a Borel measure by defining a density
p
on
[0,1]
.
Using the variables defined for
\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3}
, constants
\mathfrak{a}_{1},\mathfrak{b}_{1},\ldots
and
n
going through
1,2,\ldots
we set

p_{1}(x)=\begin{cases}\mathfrak{a}_{1}&\text{if }y_{1}<x\leq 1,\\
\mathfrak{a}_{n}&\text{if }\frac{y_{n}+z_{n}}{2}<x\leq y_{n},\\
\mathfrak{b}_{n}&\text{if }\frac{y_{n+1}+z_{n}}{2}<x\leq\frac{y_{n}+z_{n}}{2},\\
\mathfrak{a}_{n+1}&\text{if }y_{n+1}<x\leq\frac{y_{n+1}+z_{n}}{2},\\
0&\text{if }x=0.\end{cases}

and using constants
\mathfrak{c}_{n}
we furthermore define

p_{2}(x):=\begin{cases}\mathfrak{c}_{n},&\text{if }\tilde{y}_{n+1}<x\leq\tilde{y}_{n},\\
0&\text{if }x=0.\end{cases}

Finally, going through all
n\geq 2
,
1\leq m\leq N_{n}
and with the constants
\mathfrak{d}_{n,m}
let

p_{3}(x):=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }1/2<x\leq 1,\\
\mathfrak{d}_{n}&\text{if }u_{n,1}\leq x\leq\tilde{y}_{n},\\
1&\text{if }\tilde{y}_{n+1}\leq x\leq u_{n,1}\\
0&\text{if }x=0\end{cases}

and combine these to define the density
p
by

p(x):=\begin{cases}p_{1}(3x-2)&\text{if }2/3<x\leq 1,\\
p_{2}(3x-1)&\text{if }1/3<x\leq 2/3,\\
p_{3}(3x)&\text{if }0\leq x\leq 1/3.\\
\end{cases}

Now,
\mathfrak{m}=0
iff
\langle e_{n},\mathfrak{m}\rangle=E\left\langle e_{n},\phi\right\rangle=0=\langle e_{n,i}^{\prime},\mathfrak{m}\rangle=E\langle e_{n,i}^{\prime},\phi\rangle
for all
n\geq 1
,
1\leq i\leq N_{n}
.

Observe that in general, if
a,b\in[0,1]
,
a\leq b
, the density
p
is constant on
[a,b]

with value
\mu\in[0,\infty)
,
h\in\mathcal{H}
and
\psi:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
is defined by

\psi(x)=\begin{cases}(x-a)/(b-a)\mu h&x\in[a,b],\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

then for any
e_{n}
(and
e_{n,i}^{\prime}
)

\left\langle e_{n},E\psi\right\rangle=E\left\langle e_{n},\psi\right\rangle=\int_{[a,b]}\frac{x-a}{b-a}\mu\left\langle e_{n},h\right\rangle=(1/2)\mu\left\langle e_{n},h\right\rangle(b-a)

and if

\psi(x)=\begin{cases}(b-x)/(b-a)\mu h&x\in[a,b],\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

then

\left\langle e_{n},E\psi\right\rangle=(1/2)\mu\left\langle e_{n},h\right\rangle(b-a).

So,

\left\langle e_{1},\mathfrak{m}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{6}\left\langle e_{1},\left(1-\frac{y_{1}+z_{1}}{2}\right)\mathfrak{a}_{1}a_{1}+\left(\frac{y_{1}-y_{2}}{2}\right)\mathfrak{b}_{1}b_{1}+(\tilde{y}_{1}-\tilde{y}_{2})\mathfrak{c}_{1}c_{1,1}\right\rangle

will be zero by setting

\mathfrak{b}_{1}:=2\cdot 6\left(\left(1-\frac{y_{1}+z_{1}}{2}\right)\mathfrak{a}_{1}\frac{\left\langle a_{1},e_{1}\right\rangle}{\left\langle-b_{1},e_{1}\right\rangle}+\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\right)\mathfrak{c}_{1}\frac{\left\langle c_{1,1},e_{1}\right\rangle}{\left\langle-b_{1},e_{1}\right\rangle}\right)

and
\left\langle e_{n},\mathfrak{m}\right\rangle=0
by setting

\mathfrak{b}_{n}=2(n+1)(n+2)\left(\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{1}{n}-\frac{1}{n+2}\right)\mathfrak{a}_{n}\frac{\left\langle a_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle}{\left\langle-b_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle}+\left(\frac{1}{n}-\frac{1}{n+1}\right)\frac{\mathfrak{c}_{n}\beta_{n}}{\left\langle-b_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle}\right).

Also, for any
n\geq 1
we have that
\mathfrak{b}_{n}>0
if
\mathfrak{a}_{n},\mathfrak{c}_{n}>0
.
Let,
\mathcal{N}
be a normalising constant to be defined below and let

\mathfrak{a}_{1}:=\left.\frac{\mathcal{N}\left\langle-b_{1},e_{1}\right\rangle}{24\left\langle a_{1},e_{1}\right\rangle}\middle/\left(1-\frac{y_{1}+z_{1}}{2}\right)\right.>0\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{c}_{1}:=\frac{\mathcal{N}\left\langle-b_{1},e_{1}\right\rangle}{12\left\langle c_{1,1},e_{1}\right\rangle}>0

such that
\mathfrak{b}_{1}=\mathcal{N}
. Also set for all
n\geq 2

\mathfrak{a}_{n}:=\frac{n}{n+1}\frac{\mathcal{N}\left\langle-b_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle}{\left\langle a_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle}>0\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{c}_{n}:=\frac{n}{2(n+2)}\frac{\mathcal{N}\left\langle b_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle}{\beta_{n}}\frac{\Delta_{n+1}}{\Delta_{n}}>0

which makes
\mathfrak{b}_{n}=\mathcal{N}(1-\Delta_{n+1}/\Delta_{n})
and all
\left\langle e_{n},\mathfrak{m}\right\rangle=0
.
For the elements
\tilde{e}_{n,i}
we have that

6\left\langle\tilde{e}_{2,1},\mathfrak{m}\right\rangle=\Delta_{1}\mathfrak{c}_{1}\left\langle c_{1,1},\tilde{e}_{2,1}\right\rangle+\Delta_{2}\mathfrak{c}_{2}\left\langle c_{2,1},\tilde{e}_{2,1}\right\rangle+\Delta_{2}\mathfrak{d}_{2}\left\langle d_{2},\tilde{e}_{2,1}\right\rangle

and we set

\mathfrak{d}_{2}:=\frac{\Delta_{1}}{\Delta_{2}}\mathfrak{c}_{1}\frac{\left\langle c_{1,1},\tilde{e}_{2,1}\right\rangle}{\left\langle-d_{2},\tilde{e}_{2,1}\right\rangle}+\mathfrak{c}_{2}\frac{\left\langle c_{2,1},\tilde{e}_{2,1}\right\rangle}{\left\langle-d_{2},\tilde{e}_{2,1}\right\rangle}>0.

Furthermore, for all
n>2
let

\mathfrak{d}_{n}:=\frac{\Delta_{n-1}}{\Delta_{n}}\mathfrak{c}_{n-1}\frac{\left\langle-c_{n-1,N_{n-1}},\tilde{e}_{n,1}\right\rangle}{\left\langle d_{n},\tilde{e}_{n,1}\right\rangle}-\mathfrak{c}_{n}\frac{\left\langle c_{n,1},\tilde{e}_{n,1}\right\rangle}{\left\langle d_{n},\tilde{e}_{n,1}\right\rangle}.

\mathfrak{d}_{n}>0
for
n>2
since

\displaystyle\frac{2\left\langle-d_{n},\tilde{e}_{n,1}\right\rangle}{\alpha_{n,1}\mathcal{N}}\mathfrak{d}_{n}=

\displaystyle\frac{(n-1)^{2}}{n+1}\left\langle-b_{n-1},e_{n-1}\right\rangle\left\lceil\frac{2}{(n-1)\left\langle-b_{n-1},e_{n-1}\right\rangle}\right\rceil

\displaystyle-\frac{\Delta_{n+1}}{\Delta_{n}}\frac{n^{2}}{n+2}\left\langle-b_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle\left\lceil\frac{2}{n\left\langle-b_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle}\right\rceil

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2\frac{n-1}{n+1}-3\frac{n}{n+2}\frac{\Delta_{n+1}}{\Delta_{n}}=2\frac{n-1}{n+1}-3\frac{n^{2}}{(n+2)^{2}}\frac{N_{n}}{N_{n+1}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2\frac{n-1}{n+1}-\frac{3}{2}\frac{n^{2}(n+1)}{(n+2)^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{2^{n+1}}\right)

which is strictly greater zero if

\displaystyle 4(n-1)(n+2)^{2}-4n(n+1)^{2}=4(n^{2}+n-4)

is. But this is obvious for
n\geq 3
. For all remaining
n,i\geq 2
we can observe that

\left\langle\tilde{e}_{n,i},\mathfrak{m}\right\rangle=(1/6)\Delta_{n}\mathfrak{c}_{n}\left\langle c_{n,i-1}-c_{n,i},\tilde{e}_{n,i}\right\rangle=0

for all
m\geq 2
. Hence,
\mathfrak{m}=0
and the density is strictly greater
0
on all but three points. It remains to set
\mathcal{N}
such that the density integrates to one. We have for any
\mathcal{N}>0
that

\displaystyle 0<\int_{[0,1]}p=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}_{1}(1-(y_{1}+z_{1})/2)/3+(1/3)\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\mathfrak{a}_{n}((y_{n}-z_{n-1})-(y_{n}-z_{n}))/2

\displaystyle+(1/3)\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathfrak{b}_{n}((y_{n}-z_{n})-(y_{n+1}-z_{n}))/2

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{3n(n+1)}\mathfrak{c}_{n}+1/6+(1/3)\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\Delta_{n}\mathfrak{d}_{n}+(1/3)\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(u_{n,1}-\tilde{y}_{n+1}).

The first sum is a finite multiple of
\mathcal{N}
since
\mathfrak{a}_{n}\approx 2^{-n}
and the sum over

((y_{n}-z_{n-1})-(y_{n}-z_{n}))/2
is bounded by
1
. Similarly, the
\mathfrak{c}_{n}
sum is bounded since

\mathfrak{c}_{n}
itself is upper bounded by
\mathcal{N}
and the rest is quadratic in
n
. Furthermore,

\mathfrak{b}_{n}
is upper bounded by
\mathcal{N}
and the sum of the intervals cannot exceed
1
. Finally,

\mathfrak{d}_{n}
is upper bounded since

\frac{2\left\langle-d_{n},\tilde{e}_{n,1}\right\rangle}{\alpha_{n,1}\mathcal{N}}\mathfrak{d}_{n}\leq\frac{2(n-1)}{n+1}

\alpha_{n,1}
is bounded and so is
\left\langle-d_{n},\tilde{e}_{n,1}\right\rangle
. Hence, the sum is a finite multiple of
\mathcal{N}
and we have in total a term that is a finite multiple of
\mathcal{N}
plus a constant that is smaller
than
1/2
. Therefore, we can choose
\mathcal{N}
such that
\int p=1
.

(d) Behaviour of the algorithm:

Initialize the algorithm with
w_{1}:=c_{1,1}\in\phi[X]
and let
x_{t}
be the element which is chosen at stage
t
. The algorithm behaves as follows:

(1)

For any
t\geq 1
, if
w_{t}\not=0
then
x_{t}\in\{a_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}\cup\{b_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}\cup\{c_{n,m}:1\leq n,1\leq m\leq N_{n}\}\cup\{d_{n,m}:2\leq n,1\leq m\leq N_{n}\}
.

(2)

Let
n=\min\{m:a_{m}\text{ has not been chosen in steps }1\ldots t-1\}
. If
t\geq 2
then
either the smallest element of
\{(m,j):c_{m,j}\text{ has not been chosen in steps }1\ldots t-1\}\backslash\{c_{1,1}\}

in the lexicographic order is
(n,i)
with
1\leq i\leq N_{n}
and

w_{t}=-\gamma_{1}e_{1}-\ldots-\gamma_{n-1}e_{n-1}+\gamma_{n}e_{n}+\alpha_{n,i}\tilde{e}_{n,i},

where

\gamma_{j}=(2^{j}a_{j}^{\prime}-l)2^{-j},l\in\mathbb{N}\text{\quad and \quad}a_{j}^{\prime}\geq\gamma_{j}\geq\min\left\{a_{j}^{\prime},\max\left\{\frac{2^{j}\left\langle a_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle}{n}-2^{-j},0\right\}\right\}

for
1\leq j\leq n-1
and
\gamma_{n}=-(i-1)\beta_{n}
(first case),
or the smallest element is
(n+1,1)
and

w_{t}=-\gamma_{1}e_{1}-\ldots-\gamma_{n-1}e_{n-1}+\gamma_{n}e_{n}+\alpha_{n+1,1}\tilde{e}_{n+1,1},

with
\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{n-1}
like above and
\gamma_{n}=1/n
(second case).
In particular
w_{t}\not=0
.

(3)

Let
N(n):=\lceil 1+\log_{2}(n\ln(n+1))\rceil
then
n-1\geq N(n)
for all
n\geq 7
. If
n
is the smallest index of an
a_{n}
which has not been chosen yet and if this
n\geq 7
then
for any
i
with
n-1\geq i\geq N(n)

\left\langle e_{i},w_{t}\right\rangle\leq-\frac{1}{\ln(n+1)}.

(4)

For each
n\geq 1
there exists a step
t\geq 1
with
x_{t}=a_{n}
.

PPP
(
\alpha
) (1) is saying that no point on the line from
0
to an
a_{n}
,
b_{n},c_{n,m}
or
d_{n,m}
is chosen that differs from
a_{n},b_{n},c_{n,m}
and
d_{n,m}
. To see this first observe that only points
\phi(x)
will be chosen at any
stage
t
for which
\langle w_{t},\phi(x)\rangle>0
: By assumption
w_{t}\not=0
. If there exists an
e_{n}
with
\left\langle e_{n},w_{t}\right\rangle\not=0
then either
\left\langle a_{n},w_{t}\right\rangle
or

\left\langle b_{n},w_{t}\right\rangle
is strictly positive. Also, if there is an
\tilde{e}_{n,m}
,
(n,m)\not=(2,1)
, such that
\left\langle\tilde{e}_{n,m},w_{t}\right\rangle>0
then

\left\langle d_{n,m},w_{t}\right\rangle
is strictly positive. Similarly, if
\left\langle\tilde{e}_{2,1},w_{t}\right\rangle<0
then
\left\langle d_{2,1},w_{t}\right\rangle>0
. Assuming that none of these cases apply we have that
either
\left\langle\tilde{e}_{2,1},w_{t}\right\rangle>0
or there is an
\tilde{e}_{n,m}
,
(n,m)\not=(2,1)
, with
\left\langle\tilde{e}_{n,m},w_{t}\right\rangle<0
.
In the first case
\left\langle c_{1,1},w_{t}\right\rangle>0
.
In the latter case and with
\left\langle\tilde{e}_{2,1},w_{t}\right\rangle=0

let
(n^{\prime},m^{\prime}):=\min\{(n,m):\left\langle\tilde{e}_{n,m},w_{t}\right\rangle<0\}
where the minimum is taken wrt. the lexicographic ordering. We have

\left\langle c_{n^{\prime},m^{\prime}-1},w_{t}\right\rangle=\alpha_{n^{\prime},m^{\prime}-1}\left\langle\tilde{e}_{n^{\prime},m^{\prime}-1},w_{t}\right\rangle-\alpha_{n^{\prime},m^{\prime}}\left\langle\tilde{e}_{n^{\prime},m^{\prime}},w_{t}\right\rangle>-\alpha_{n^{\prime},m^{\prime}}\left\langle\tilde{e}_{n^{\prime},m^{\prime}},w_{t}\right\rangle>0
, if
m^{\prime}>1
, and if
m^{\prime}=1
then

\langle c_{n^{\prime}-1,N_{n^{\prime}-1}},w_{t}\rangle=\alpha_{n^{\prime}-1,N_{n^{\prime}-1}}\langle\tilde{e}_{n^{\prime}-1,N_{n^{\prime}-1}},w_{t}\rangle-\alpha_{n^{\prime},1}\left\langle\tilde{e}_{n^{\prime},1},w_{t}\right\rangle>-\alpha_{n^{\prime},1}\langle\tilde{e}_{n^{\prime},1},w_{t}\rangle>0.

If the chosen
\phi(x)
is on the line from
0
to an
a_{n}
then
a_{n}=\xi\phi(x)
with
\xi\geq 1
and

0<\left\langle\phi(x),w_{t}\right\rangle\leq\xi\left\langle\phi(x),w_{t}\right\rangle=\left\langle a_{n},w_{t}\right\rangle
and
\phi(x)=a_{n}
. The same argument applies to
b_{n},c_{n,m}
and
d_{n,m}
.

(
\beta
) We
prove by induction
over
t\geq 2
that (2) holds.
We start with the
induction basis
.
w_{1}=c_{1,1}=e_{1}+\alpha_{2,1}\tilde{e}_{2,1}
and
we have
\left\langle w_{1},b_{n}\right\rangle\leq 0
,
\left\langle w_{1},d_{n}\right\rangle\leq 0
for all
n
,
\left\langle w_{1},a_{n}\right\rangle=0
for all
n\geq 2
,

\left\langle w_{1},c_{n,i}\right\rangle=0
if either
n>2
or (
n=2
and
i>2
). Furthermore,

\displaystyle\left\langle w_{1},c_{1,1}\right\rangle=\left\lVert c_{1,1}\right\rVert^{2}=1+\alpha_{2,1}^{2}=1+\frac{\left\langle e_{2},a_{2}\right\rangle}{2}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{C}{8}\left\lceil\frac{4}{\ln(3)}\right\rceil+\frac{1}{4}\leq 1+\frac{C}{4}\left\lceil\frac{2}{\ln(2)}\right\rceil+\frac{1}{4}

\displaystyle<1+\frac{C}{2}\left\lceil\frac{2}{\ln(2)}\right\rceil=\left\langle w_{1},a_{1}\right\rangle

since
C\geq 1
,
\lceil 2/\ln(2)\rceil=3
and hence

\frac{C}{4}\left\lceil\frac{2}{\ln(2)}\right\rceil>\frac{1}{4}.

Also,

\left\langle w_{1},c_{2,1}\right\rangle=\alpha_{2,1}^{2}<\left\langle w_{1},c_{1,1}\right\rangle<\left\langle w_{1},a_{1}\right\rangle

and
x_{1}=a_{1}
. Therefore,

w_{2}=w_{1}-a_{1}=e_{1}+\alpha_{2,1}\tilde{e}_{2,1}-(a_{1}^{\prime}+1)e_{1}=-a_{1}^{\prime}e_{1}+\alpha_{2,1}\tilde{e}_{2,1}

and
w_{2}
has the promised form.

(
\gamma
) Next, we address the
induction step
.

(i)

Assuming
w_{t}
has the given form in step
t
we can observe that

\left\langle x_{t},w_{t}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\left\langle a_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle}{n}>0

since in the first case

\left\langle c_{n,i},w_{t}\right\rangle=\beta_{n}\gamma_{n}+\alpha_{n,i}^{2}=-(i-1)\beta_{n}^{2}+(\alpha_{n,1}^{2}+(i-1)\beta_{n}^{2})=\frac{\left\langle e_{n},a_{n}\right\rangle}{n}

in case that
i>1
or, for
i=1
,

\left\langle c_{n,i},w_{t}\right\rangle=\alpha_{n,1}^{2}=\frac{\left\langle e_{n},a_{n}\right\rangle}{n}.

In the second case,

\left\langle a_{n},w_{t}\right\rangle\geq\gamma_{n}\left\langle e_{n},a_{n}\right\rangle=\frac{\left\langle e_{n},a_{n}\right\rangle}{n}.

(ii)
For the
b_{j}

(i)
implies that, first, no
j\geq n
will have been chosen in
t
since for these
\left\langle b_{j},w_{t}\right\rangle\leq 0
holds in both cases. Also, if for a
j
,
1\leq j\leq n-1
,
\gamma_{j}<2^{j}\left\langle a_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle/n
then

\left\langle b_{j},w_{t}\right\rangle=\gamma_{j}2^{-j}<\left\langle a_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle/n
and
b_{j}\not=x_{t}
.
On the other hand, if
\gamma_{j}\geq 2^{j}\left\langle a_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle/n
and
x_{t}=b_{j}
then
the coefficient changes by
-2^{-j}
, i.e. the new coefficient is

\gamma_{j}-2^{-j}\geq 2^{j}\frac{\left\langle a_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle}{n}-2^{-j}.

The coefficient is also always non-negative since
\gamma_{j}
is a multiple of
2^{-j}
and
b_{j}
will not be selected if
\gamma_{j}=0
. In total, all cases are consistent with our induction hypothesis and we are safe against any application of
b_{j}
.

(iii)
In terms of
a_{j}
, we can directly observe that
\left\langle a_{j},w_{t}\right\rangle=0
if
j>n
and
\left\langle a_{j},w_{t}\right\rangle=-\gamma_{j}\left\langle e_{j},a_{j}\right\rangle\leq 0
if
j<n
. So only

a_{n}
might have been chosen at time
t
. However, in the first case we have
that

\left\langle a_{n},w_{t}\right\rangle=\gamma_{n}\left\langle a_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle=-(i-1)\beta_{n}\left\langle a_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle\leq\frac{N_{n}-1}{nN_{n}}\left\langle a_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle<\frac{\left\langle a_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle}{n}

and
x_{t}\not=a_{n}
. In the second case, if
\gamma_{n}=1/n
, then

\left\langle a_{n},w_{t}\right\rangle=\frac{\left\langle a_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle}{n}.

Thus
a_{n}
might be chosen, and, in case it is, then the new coefficient is

\gamma_{n}-a_{n}^{\prime}-1/n=-a_{n}^{\prime}
which is consistent with the induction hypothesis.

(iv)
Turning to the
c_{n^{\prime},i^{\prime}}
elements we can observe that for
(n,i)>(2,1)
we have
\left\langle w_{t},c_{1,1}\right\rangle\leq 0
.
For
(n,i)=(2,1)
we are in the first case since
N_{2}=4
and

\displaystyle\left\langle w_{t},c_{1,1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle-\gamma_{1}e_{1}+\gamma_{2}e_{2}+\alpha_{2,1}\tilde{e}_{2,1},e_{1}+\alpha_{2,1}\tilde{e}_{2,1}\right\rangle=-\gamma_{1}+(1/2)\left\langle e_{2},a_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq-(1/2)\left\langle e_{2},a_{2}\right\rangle+1/2+(1/2)\left\langle e_{2},a_{2}\right\rangle.

But,
1/2<(1/2)\left\langle a_{2},e_{2}\right\rangle
and
c_{1,1}
will never be chosen. For the remaining
c_{n^{\prime},i^{\prime}}
elements we have in the first case in step
t
that no
c_{n^{\prime},i^{\prime}}
will be chosen if
n^{\prime}\not=n
or
i^{\prime}\not=i
since
in these cases

\left\langle w_{t},c_{n^{\prime},i^{\prime}}\right\rangle\begin{cases}=0&\text{if }n^{\prime}>n,\\
=\gamma_{n}\beta_{n}=-(i-1)\beta_{n}^{2}\leq 0&\text{if }n^{\prime}=n\text{ and }(i^{\prime}>i\text{ or }i^{\prime}<i-1),\\
=\gamma_{n}\beta_{n}-\alpha_{n,i}^{2}<0&\text{if }n^{\prime}=n\text{ and }i^{\prime}+1=i,\\
\leq 0&\text{if }2\leq n^{\prime}=n-1\text{ and }i^{\prime}=N_{n^{\prime}}\text{ and }i=1,\quad(*)\\
=-\gamma_{n^{\prime}}\beta_{n^{\prime}}<\left\langle w_{t},b_{n^{\prime}}\right\rangle&\text{if }n^{\prime}<n\text{ and }(*)\text{ does not apply.}\end{cases}

(*) follows for
n\geq 3
from

\displaystyle\left\langle w_{t},c_{n-1,N_{n-1}}\right\rangle=\left\langle w_{t},\beta_{n-1}e_{n-1}+\alpha_{n-1,N_{n-1}}\tilde{e}_{n-1,N_{n-1}}-\alpha_{n,1}\tilde{e}_{n,1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\gamma_{n-1}\beta_{n-1}-\alpha^{2}_{n,1}=\frac{\gamma_{n-1}}{(n-1)N_{n-1}}-\alpha^{2}_{n,1}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{n-1}2^{-n}-\frac{\left\langle e_{n},a_{n}\right\rangle}{n}\leq 2^{-n}a_{n-1}^{\prime}-\frac{a_{n}^{\prime}}{n}

\displaystyle=C\left(2^{-n}\left\lceil\frac{2^{n-1}}{\ln(n)}\right\rceil 2^{-(n-1)}-\frac{1}{n}\left\lceil\frac{2^{n}}{\ln(n+1)}\right\rceil 2^{-n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq Cn^{-1}2^{-2n-1}\left(n\left\lceil\frac{2^{n}}{\ln(n^{2})}\right\rceil-2^{n-1}\left\lceil\frac{2^{n}}{\ln(n+1)}\right\rceil\right)\leq 0.

If now
x_{t}=c_{n,i}
then, in case
i<N_{n}
,

\displaystyle w_{t+1}

\displaystyle=w_{t}-c_{n,i}=-\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\gamma_{i}e_{i}-(i-1)\beta_{n}e_{n}+\alpha_{n,i}\tilde{e}_{n,i}-\beta_{n}e_{n}-\alpha_{n,i}\tilde{e}_{n,i}+\alpha_{n,i+1}\tilde{e}_{n,i+1}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\gamma_{i}e_{i}-i\beta_{n}e_{n}+\alpha_{n,i+1}\tilde{e}_{n,i+1}

which has the desired form. Similarly, in case that
i=N_{n}

\displaystyle w_{t+1}

\displaystyle=w_{t}-c_{n,N_{n}}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\gamma_{i}e_{i}-(N_{n}-1)\beta_{n}e_{n}+\alpha_{n,N_{n}}\tilde{e}_{n,N_{n}}-\beta_{n}e_{n}-\alpha_{n,N_{n}}\tilde{e}_{n,N_{n}}+\alpha_{n+1,1}\tilde{e}_{n+1,1}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\gamma_{i}e_{i}-N_{n}\beta_{n}e_{n}+\alpha_{n+1,1}\tilde{e}_{n+1,1}

which has the form of the second case since
-N_{n}\beta_{n}=1/n
.

In the second case no element
c_{n^{\prime},i^{\prime}}
will be chosen since

\left\langle w_{t},c_{n^{\prime},i^{\prime}}\right\rangle=\begin{cases}0&\text{if }n^{\prime}>n+1\text{ or }(n^{\prime}=n+1\text{ and }i^{\prime}>1),\\
\alpha_{n+1,1}^{2}<\left\langle e_{n},a_{n}\right\rangle/n&\text{if }n^{\prime}=n+1\text{ and }i^{\prime}=1,\\
\beta_{n}/n-\alpha_{n+1,1}^{2}<0&\text{if }n^{\prime}=n\text{ and }i^{\prime}=N_{n},\\
\beta_{n}/n<0&\text{if }n^{\prime}=n\text{ and }i^{\prime}<N_{n},\\
-\gamma_{n^{\prime}}\beta_{n^{\prime}}<\left\langle w_{t},b_{n^{\prime}}\right\rangle\text{ or }=0&\text{if }n^{\prime}<n.\\
\end{cases}

(v)
We turn to the
d
elements. First case: If
(n,i)=(2,1)
then
\left\langle w_{t},d_{2}\right\rangle=-(1/2)\alpha_{2,1}^{2}<0
and otherwise

\left\langle w_{t},d_{2}\right\rangle=0
. In either way
d_{2}
will not be chosen. For any other
n^{\prime}
we have that

\left\langle w_{t},d_{n^{\prime}}\right\rangle=0
if
(n,i)\not=(n^{\prime},1)
. Otherwise

\displaystyle\left\langle w_{t},d_{n^{\prime}}\right\rangle=(1/2)\alpha_{n,1}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\left\langle e_{n},a_{n}\right\rangle}{n}

and
\left\langle w_{t},d_{n^{\prime}}\right\rangle<\left\langle a_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle/n
and
d_{n^{\prime}}
will never be chosen.

Second case:
\left\langle w_{t},d_{2}\right\rangle\leq 0
and all
d_{n^{\prime}}
with
n^{\prime}\not=n+1
are zero. Finally

\left\langle w_{t},d_{n+1}\right\rangle=(1/2)\alpha_{n+1,1}^{2}=\frac{\left\langle e_{n+1},a_{n+1}\right\rangle}{n+1}<\frac{\left\langle e_{n},a_{n}\right\rangle}{n}

since the sequence
\left\langle e_{n},a_{n}\right\rangle
is non-increasing.

So in step
t+1
the element
w_{t+1}
will have the right form, and, certainly,
w_{t+1}\not=0
.

(\delta)
Next we prove
(3)
. Since the smallest index
n
for which
a_{n}
has not been chosen in rounds
1
to
t
is assumed to be larger than
7
we can assume that
t\geq 2
.

For each
m
,
\left\langle a_{m},e_{m}\right\rangle/m\geq a_{m}^{\prime}/m\geq 1/(m\ln(m+1))
. Hence, from (2) we conclude for all
i
with

n-1\geq i\geq N(n)

\displaystyle-\langle e_{i},w_{t+1}\rangle

\displaystyle=\gamma_{i}\geq\min\left\{a_{i}^{\prime},\max\left\{2^{i}\frac{\left\langle a_{n},e_{n}\right\rangle}{n}-2^{-i},0\right\}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\min\left\{\frac{1}{\ln(i+1)},\max\left\{\frac{2^{i}}{n\ln(n+1)}-2^{-i},0\right\}\right\}.

Using the assumption
i\geq N(n)
we observe that

i\geq 1+\log_{2}(n\ln(n+1))=\log_{2}(2n\ln(n+1))\Rightarrow 2^{i}\geq 2n\ln(n+1)\Rightarrow\frac{2^{i}}{n\ln(n+1)}-1\geq 1

and since
i>1

\frac{2^{i}}{n\ln(n+1)}-2^{-i}\geq\frac{2^{i}}{n\ln(n+1)}-1\geq\frac{1}{\ln(i+1)}\geq\frac{1}{\ln(n+1)}.

So, for
n-1\geq i\geq N(n)
,

-\langle e_{i},w_{t+1}\rangle\geq\frac{1}{\ln(n+1)}.

(
\epsilon
)

(4)
also follows from (2).
First, if
a_{n}
has been chosen in any step
t
then
for all
t^{\prime}>t
(2) tells us that
\left\langle a_{n},w_{t^{\prime}}\right\rangle\leq 0
. Consequently,
x_{t^{\prime}}\not=a_{n}
and
a_{n}
will
be chosen at most ones. Also, if
a_{n}
is the element with minimal index which has not been chosen yet in time
t
then
either
\left\langle w_{t},a_{n}\right\rangle=\left\langle w_{t},a_{m}\right\rangle=0
or
\left\langle w_{t},a_{n}\right\rangle>\left\langle w_{t},a_{m}\right\rangle
for all
m>n
. In the first case no element
a_{n^{\prime}}
will be chosen and in the second case, if an
a_{n^{\prime}}

will be chosen it will be the one with the smallest index in the set of elements which have not been chosen yet. So the elements
a_{n}
will be chosen in order and no element will be skipped.

Let us now assume that
\{a_{m}:a_{m}\not=x_{t}\text{ for all }t\geq 1\}
is not empty and let

a_{n}
be the element with the smallest index in this set.

The argument in (
\gamma
) shows us that no
c_{m,j}
with
m>n
will be chosen.
Also, if
c_{m,j}
,
m\leq n
, has been chosen in any step
t
then for all
t^{\prime}>t
we again infer from (2)
and the argument in (
\gamma
) that
c_{m,j}
will not be chosen in
t^{\prime}
and, hence, each
c_{m,j}
is not chosen more than ones. Also none of the
d_{n,i}
elements will be ever chosen.

So the only way that an
a_{n}
is never chosen is that infinite many
b_{m}
elements are selected. Yet,
no
b_{m}
with
m\geq n
will be chosen since the inner product with the weight vector is less or equal to zero. Also
each
b_{m}
,
m<n
can only be chosen finite many times before the weight vector in direction
e_{m}
becomes
0
and the inner product with
b_{m}
becomes
0
too (at which point it will certainly not be chosen any more). So only finite many applications of
b_{m}
’s are possible with a contradiction that
a_{n}
will not be chosen.

QQQ

(e) Unboundedness:

d.3 and d.4 allow us now to show that the sequence
\{\left\lVert w_{t}\right\rVert\}_{t\geq 1}
is unbounded. Assume that at stage
t
the element
n
is the smallest index such that

\left\langle a_{n},w_{t}\right\rangle
is positive.

For
n\geq 7
we know from
d.3
that
\left\lvert\left\langle e_{i},w_{t}\right\rangle\right\rvert\geq 1/\ln(n+1)
for all
i
,
N(n)\leq i<n

Hence, for
n\geq 7
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert w_{t}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\left\lvert\langle e_{i},w_{t}\rangle\right\rvert^{2}\geq\sum_{i=N(n)}^{n-1}\frac{1}{(\ln(n+1))^{2}}\geq\frac{n-1-N(n)}{(\ln(n+1))^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{n-1-\lceil 1+\log_{2}(n\ln(n+1))\rceil}{(\ln(n+1))^{2}}\geq\frac{n-3}{(\ln(n+1))^{2}}-\frac{\log_{2}(n\ln(n+1))}{(\ln(n+1))^{2}}.

Furthermore,

\frac{\log_{2}(n\ln(n+1))}{(\ln(n+1))^{2}}=\frac{\ln(n)+\ln(\ln(n+1)))}{\ln(2)(\ln(n+1))^{2}}\leq\frac{2}{\ln(2)},

since
\ln(x)\leq x
for all
x>0
and
\ln(n+1)>0
. Hence,

\left\lVert w_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\geq\frac{n-3}{(\ln(n+1))^{2}}-\frac{2}{\ln(2)}.

The right side goes to infinity in
n
and, since for every
n
there is a
t
at which
a_{n}
is chosen
due to d.4, the norm of
w_{t}
crosses any boundary at one time
t
.
∎

Corollary 2
.

There exists a continuous kernel on
[0,1]
, a Borel probability measure on
[0,1]
which assigns positive measure to open subsets of
[0,1]
and an initialization for which the algorithm does not converge with a
1/t
rate to
\mathfrak{m}
.

Proof.

We consider the Hilbert space
(\mathcal{H},\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle)
from Proposition
6
with the corresponding feature map
\phi:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
. Define the continuous kernel function
k(x,y):=\left\langle\phi(x),\phi(y)\right\rangle
on
[0,1]
and let the corresponding RKHS be
(\mathcal{K},(\cdot,\cdot))
.
The geometry of the two spaces is closely related. We have for scalars
a_{i},b_{j}
and
x_{i},y_{j}\in[0,1]
,
i=1,\ldots,n,j=1,\ldots,m
, that

\displaystyle\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\phi(x_{i}),\sum_{j=1}^{m}b_{j}\phi(y_{j})\right\rangle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}k(x_{i},y_{j})=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}k(x_{i},\cdot),\sum_{j=1}^{m}b_{j}k(y_{j},\cdot)\right).

Furthermore, we know that the Bochner-integral
\mathfrak{m}\in\mathcal{H}
lies in
\text{{cch}\,}\phi[\mathcal{X}]
which equals the closure of
\text{{ch}\,}\phi[\mathcal{X}]

[
30
]
[Thm. 5.2, p.71]
and there exists a sequence
\{n_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
,
n_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
, elements
x_{ij}\in[0,1]
, and non-negative weights
a_{ij}
with
\sum_{j=1}^{n_{i}}a_{ij}=1
such that the sequence

\{s_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{n_{i}}a_{ij}\phi(x_{ij})\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to
\mathfrak{m}
in norm, i.e.
\left\lVert\mathfrak{m}-s_{i}\right\rVert\rightarrow 0
for
i\rightarrow\infty
. The corresponding sequence
\{\bar{s}_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{n_{i}}a_{ij}k(x_{ij},\cdot))\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
\mathcal{K}
since

\left\lVert\bar{s}_{i}-\bar{s}_{j}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{K}}=\left\lVert s_{i}-s_{j}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}}

and has a limit
\mathfrak{n}\in\mathcal{K}
because
\mathcal{K}
is complete. In particular, for any
x\in\mathcal{X}

\displaystyle\left\lvert\left(\mathfrak{n},k(x,\cdot)\right)-\left\langle\mathfrak{m},\phi(x)\right\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert(\mathfrak{n}-\bar{s}_{i},k(x,\cdot))+(\bar{s}_{i},k(x,\cdot))-\left\langle s_{i},\phi(x)\right\rangle+\left\langle s_{i}-\mathfrak{m},\phi(x)\right\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq k(x,x)\left\lVert\mathfrak{n}-\bar{s}_{i}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{K}}+\left\lVert\phi(x)\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}}\left\lVert\mathfrak{m}-s_{i}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}}\rightarrow 0\text{ (in }i)

and
(\mathfrak{n},k(x,\cdot))=\left\langle\mathfrak{m},\phi(x)\right\rangle
for every
x\in X
.
Furthermore, for arbitrary
l
points
x_{1},\ldots,x_{l}\in X
and scalars
a_{1},\ldots,a_{l}
it holds that
(\mathfrak{n},\sum_{i=1}^{l}a_{i}k(x_{i},\cdot))=\langle\mathfrak{m},\sum_{i=1}^{l}a_{i}\phi(x_{i})\rangle
and

\displaystyle\left\lvert\left\lVert\mathfrak{n}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{K}}-\left\lVert\mathfrak{m}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq|\left\lVert\mathfrak{n}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{K}}-\left\lVert\bar{s}_{i}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{K}}\!|+|\left\lVert\bar{s}_{i}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{K}}-\left\lVert s_{i}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}}\!|+|\left\lVert s_{i}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}}-\left\lVert\mathfrak{m}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}}\!|

which also goes to
0
in
i
and therefore
\left\lVert\mathfrak{n}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{K}}=\left\lVert\mathfrak{m}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}}
.

The function
k(x,\cdot):X\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
is continuous and therefore Bochner-integrable with respect to
P
. Denote the Bochner integral with
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}=\int k(x,\cdot)\,dP
.
For any
x\in X

\mathfrak{n}(x)=(\mathfrak{n},k(x,\cdot))=\left\langle\mathfrak{m},\phi(x)\right\rangle=E\left\langle\phi(\cdot),\phi(x)\right\rangle=Ek(x,\cdot)=(\mathfrak{n}^{\prime},k(x,\cdot))=\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}(x)

and
\mathfrak{n}=\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}
.

Now if the algorithm is applied in
(\mathcal{K},(\cdot,\cdot))
with the initialization
k(x_{0},\cdot)
, where
x_{0}
is the element in
\mathcal{X}
that maps to the initialization
\phi(x_{0})
that we use in Proposition
6
, then sequences of elements
x_{t}
and of weights
w_{t}
are generated. The weights
w_{t}
are of the form
k(x_{0},\cdot)+\sum_{i=1}^{t}k(x_{i},\cdot)-t\mathfrak{n}
. The sequence
x_{1},x_{2},\ldots
also
maximizes the objective in
(\mathcal{H},\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle)
. This can be seen by an induction over the weights
\tilde{w}_{t}\in\mathcal{H}
that are generated by the algorithm. The induction step is the following.

\displaystyle\max_{x\in[0,1]}\left\langle\tilde{w}_{t},\phi(x)\right\rangle=\max_{x\in[0,1]}\left(\left\langle\phi(x_{0}),\phi(x)\right\rangle+\sum_{i=1}^{t}\left\langle\phi(x_{i}),\phi(x)\right\rangle-t\left\langle\mathfrak{m},\phi(x)\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=\max_{x\in[0,1]}\left((k(x_{0},\cdot),k(x,\cdot))+\sum_{i=1}^{t}(k(x_{i},\cdot),k(x,\cdot))-t(\mathfrak{n},k(x,\cdot))\right)=(w_{t},k(x_{t+1},\cdot))

\displaystyle=(k(x_{0},\cdot),k(x_{t+1},\cdot))+\sum_{i=1}^{t}(k(x_{i},\cdot),k(x_{t+1},\cdot))-t(\mathfrak{n},k(x_{t+1},\cdot))

\displaystyle=\Bigl\langle\phi(x_{0})+\sum_{i=1}^{t}\phi(x_{i})-t\mathfrak{m},\phi(x_{t+1})\Bigr\rangle=\left\langle\tilde{w}_{t},\phi(x_{t+1})\right\rangle.

From Proposition
6
we can now infer that the sequence
\{\left\lVert\tilde{w}_{t}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{H}}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}}=\{\left\lVert w_{t}\right\rVert_{\mathcal{K}}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
is unbounded and the algorithm does not converge with the fast rate in
\mathcal{K}
.
∎
